          CSVFormat format = CSVFormat.DEFAULT.withNullString("NULL");
          format.print(null, output, true);
          assertEquals("NULL", output.toString().trim());

    test_name: testPrintWithSpecialCharacters
    test_code: |
      @Test
      public void testPrintWithSpecialCharacters() throws IOException {
          StringWriter output = new StringWriter();
          CSVFormat format = CSVFormat.DEFAULT.withEscape(BACKSLASH);
          format.print("Value, with special; characters", output, true);
          assertTrue(output.toString().contains("Value\\, with special\\; characters"));

test_designs:
  - design_name: |
      Test trimming behavior for surrounding spaces in values.
    method_context:
      method_signature_hint: |
        private CharSequence trim(final CharSequence charSequence)
      entry_hint: |
        public String format(final Object... values)
    execution_flow: |
      1. Call format() with values that include strings with leading and trailing spaces.
      2. During formatting, trim(...) is reached to process the values.
      3. The trimming behavior is applied to the values before output.
    input_focus: |
      Provide an array of strings with leading and trailing spaces, such as "  value1  ", "  value2  ".
    observable_behavior: |
      Verify that the output string does not include leading or trailing spaces around the values.

  - design_name: |
      Test handling of equal delimiter and quote character.
    method_context:
      method_signature_hint: |
        private void validate()
      entry_hint: |
        public CSVFormat withQuote(final Character quoteChar)
    execution_flow: |
      1. Call withQuote() with a quote character that is the same as the current delimiter.
      2. The validate() method is invoked to check the character constraints.
      3. An IllegalArgumentException is expected due to the character conflict.
    input_focus: |
      Set the delimiter to ',' and attempt to set the quote character to the same value.
    observable_behavior: |
      Check that an IllegalArgumentException is thrown indicating the conflict between the delimiter and quote character.

  - design_name: |
      Test handling of equal escape character and delimiter.
    method_context:
      method_signature_hint: |
        private void validate()
      entry_hint: |
        public CSVFormat withEscape(final Character escape)
    execution_flow: |
      1. Call withEscape() with an escape character that is the same as the current delimiter.
      2. The validate() method is invoked to check the character constraints.
      3. An IllegalArgumentException is expected due to the character conflict.
    input_focus: |
      Set the delimiter to ',' and attempt to set the escape character to the same value.
    observable_behavior: |
      Verify that an IllegalArgumentException is thrown indicating the conflict between the delimiter and escape character.

      Test handling of equal comment marker and delimiter.

```yaml
language: java
existing_test_function_signature: public void testPrintWithTrimming() throws IOException
new_tests:
  - test_behavior: Test trimming behavior for surrounding spaces in values.
    test_name: testFormatTrimmingBehavior
    test_code: |
      @Test
      public void testFormatTrimmingBehavior() {
          CSVFormat format = CSVFormat.DEFAULT;
          String result = format.format("  value1  ", "  value2  ");
          assertEquals("value1,value2", result); // Expecting trimmed values without surrounding spaces
      }
  - test_behavior: Test handling of equal delimiter and quote character.
    test_name: testWithEqualDelimiterAndQuoteCharacter
    test_code: |
      @Test(expected = IllegalArgumentException.class)
      public void testWithEqualDelimiterAndQuoteCharacter() {
          CSVFormat format = CSVFormat.DEFAULT.withDelimiter(',');
          format.withQuote(','); // Attempting to set quote character to the same as delimiter
      }
  - test_behavior: Test handling of equal escape character and delimiter.
    test_name: testWithEqualEscapeCharacterAndDelimiter
    test_code: |
      @Test(expected = IllegalArgumentException.class)
      public void testWithEqualEscapeCharacterAndDelimiter() {
          CSVFormat format = CSVFormat.DEFAULT.withDelimiter(',');
          format.withEscape(','); // Attempting to set escape character to the same as delimiter
      }
  - test_behavior: Test handling of equal comment marker and delimiter.
    test_name: testWithEqualCommentMarkerAndDelimiter
    test_code: |
      @Test(expected = IllegalArgumentException.class)

    test_name: testPrintRecordWithQuoteCharacter
    test_code: |
      @Test
      public void testPrintRecordWithQuoteCharacter() throws IOException {
          StringWriter output = new StringWriter();
          CSVFormat format = CSVFormat.DEFAULT.withQuote('"');
          format.printRecord(output, "Value with \"internal quote\"");
          String expectedOutput = "\"Value with \\\"internal quote\\\"\""; // Expecting the internal quote to be escaped
          assertEquals(expectedOutput, output.toString().trim());
      }
    new_imports_code: ''

  - test_behavior: Test behavior when escape character is set.
    test_name: testPrintRecordWithEscapeCharacter
    test_code: |

          StringWriter output = new StringWriter();
          CSVFormat format = CSVFormat.DEFAULT.withEscape(BACKSLASH);
          format.printRecord(output, "Value with special; characters and newline\n");
          String expectedOutput = "\"Value with special; characters and newline\\n\""; // Expecting the newline to be escaped
          assertEquals(expectedOutput, output.toString().trim());
      }
    new_imports_code: ''

          assertEquals(expectedOutput, output.toString().trim());
      }

  - design_name: |
      Test behavior when a new record is printed.
    method_context:
      method_signature_hint: |
        private void print(final Object value, final Appendable out, final boolean newRecord)
      entry_hint: |
        public void printRecord(final Appendable out, final Object... values)

      1. Call printRecord() with multiple values, ensuring the first value triggers newRecord as true.
      2. During printing, print(...) is reached where newRecord is evaluated.
      3. The path for handling the first value as a new record is executed.
    input_focus: |
      Provide a set of values to print, ensuring the first value is distinct to trigger newRecord.
    observable_behavior: |
      Verify that the output includes the correct formatting for the first value, potentially affecting subsequent values.

  - design_name: |
      Test handling of characters that require quoting.
    method_context:
      method_signature_hint: |
        private void printAndQuote(final Object object, final CharSequence value, final int offset, final int len, final Appendable out, final boolean newRecord)
      entry_hint: |
        public void printRecord(final Appendable out, final Object... values)
    execution_flow: |
      1. Call printRecord() with a value that contains special characters requiring quoting.
      2. During printing, printAndQuote(...) is reached where character evaluation occurs.
      3. The branch for handling special characters is executed.
    input_focus: |
      Use a value that includes characters like commas or quotes that necessitate quoting in CSV format.
    observable_behavior: |
      Check that the output correctly encapsulates the value with quotes and escapes any internal quotes.

      Test behavior when the record separator is set.

      method_signature_hint: |
        private void println(final Appendable out)
      entry_hint: |
        public CSVPrinter print(final Appendable out) throws IOException

        public void printRecord(final Appendable out, final Object... values)

      2. During printing, printAndEscape(...) is reached where character evaluation occurs.
      3. The branch for handling escape characters is executed.
    input_focus: |

      @Test

          StringWriter output = new StringWriter();
          CSVFormat format = CSVFormat.DEFAULT;
          format.printRecord(output, "First Value", "Second Value");
          String expectedOutput = "\"First Value\",\"Second Value\""; // Expecting both values to be printed correctly
          assertEquals(expectedOutput, output.toString().trim());
      }
    new_imports_code: ''
  
  - test_behavior: Test handling of characters that require quoting.
    test_name: testPrintRecordWithSpecialCharacters
    test_code: |
      @Test
      public void testPrintRecordWithSpecialCharacters() throws IOException {
          StringWriter output = new StringWriter();
          CSVFormat format = CSVFormat.DEFAULT;
          format.printRecord(output, "Value with, comma and \"quote\"");
          String expectedOutput = "\"Value with, comma and \\\"quote\\\"\""; // Expecting the value to be quoted and internal quotes escaped
          assertEquals(expectedOutput, output.toString().trim());
      }
    new_imports_code: ''

    test_name: testPrintWithCustomRecordSeparator

          format.printRecord(output, "Value1", "Value2");
          String expectedOutput = "\"Value1\",\"Value2\"\n"; // Expecting the record separator to be included
          assertEquals(expectedOutput, output.toString());
      }
    new_imports_code: ''

  - test_behavior: Test behavior when the escape character is set.
    test_name: testPrintRecordWithEscapeCharacter
    test_code: |

      2. Call equals() on one instance, passing the other as an argument.
      3. The comparison logic is executed, reaching the uncovered branches.
    input_focus: |
      Instantiate two CSVFormat objects, one with a comma delimiter and another with a tab delimiter.
    observable_behavior: |
      Verify that the equals method returns false when comparing formats with different delimiters.

      Test hashCode consistency for CSVFormat instances.
    method_context:
      method_signature_hint: |
        @Override public int hashCode()
      entry_hint: |
        public CSVFormat withQuote(Character quoteChar)
    execution_flow: |
      1. Create a CSVFormat instance with a specific quote character.
      2. Call hashCode() on the instance.
      3. The hashCode logic is executed, reaching uncovered branches.
    input_focus: |
      Create a CSVFormat instance with a quote character set to double quote.
    observable_behavior: |
      Check that the hashCode method produces a consistent hash value for the same instance.

      Test behavior when nullString is set and used in printing.
    method_context:
      method_signature_hint: |
        public void print(final Object value, final Appendable out, final boolean newRecord)
      entry_hint: |
        public CSVPrinter print(final Appendable out)
    execution_flow: |
      1. Create a CSVFormat instance with a specific nullString.
      2. Use the print method to output a null value.
      3. The print logic is executed, reaching the uncovered branches.
    input_focus: |
      Set the nullString to "\\N" and print a null value using the CSVPrinter.
    observable_behavior: |
      Verify that the output correctly represents the null value according to the specified nullString.

      Test escaping behavior for special characters in CSV values.

    input_focus: |
      Provide a value that includes a newline character and a comma.

language: java
existing_test_function_signature: public void testEqualsWithDifferentDelimiters()
new_tests:
  - test_behavior: Test equality behavior with different CSVFormat instances based on delimiters.
    test_name: testEqualsWithDifferentDelimiters
    test_code: |
      @Test
      public void testEqualsWithDifferentDelimiters() {
          CSVFormat format1 = CSVFormat.newFormat(',');
          CSVFormat format2 = CSVFormat.newFormat('\t');
          assertFalse(format1.equals(format2)); // Expecting false due to different delimiters
      }
    new_imports_code: ''

  - test_behavior: Test hashCode consistency for CSVFormat instances with a specific quote character.
    test_name: testHashCodeWithQuoteCharacter
    test_code: |
      @Test
      public void testHashCodeWithQuoteCharacter() {
          CSVFormat format = CSVFormat.DEFAULT.withQuote('"');
          int hashCode = format.hashCode();
          assertNotEquals(0, hashCode); // Ensure hashCode is not zero
      }
    new_imports_code: ''

  - test_behavior: Test behavior when nullString is set and used in printing.
    test_name: testPrintWithNullString
    test_code: |
      @Test
      public void testPrintWithNullString() throws IOException {
          StringWriter output = new StringWriter();
          CSVFormat format = CSVFormat.DEFAULT.withNullString("\\N");
          format.print(null, output, true);
          assertEquals("\\N", output.toString().trim()); // Expecting the output to represent null as "\\N"
      }
    new_imports_code: ''

  - test_behavior: Test escaping behavior for special characters in CSV values.
    test_name: testPrintAndEscapeSpecialCharacters
    test_code: |
      @Test
      public void testPrintAndEscapeSpecialCharacters() throws IOException {
          StringWriter output = new StringWriter();
          CSVFormat format = CSVFormat.DEFAULT.withEscape(BACKSLASH);
          format.printRecord(output, "Value with newline\nand comma,");
          String expectedOutput = "\"Value with newline\\nand comma,\""; // Expecting the newline to be escaped
          assertEquals(expectedOutput, output.toString().trim());
      }
    new_imports_code: ''

  - test_behavior: Test comment marker behavior in CSVFormat.
    test_name: testIsCommentMarkerSet
    test_code: |
      @Test
      public void testIsCommentMarkerSet() {
          CSVFormat format = CSVFormat.DEFAULT.withCommentMarker('#');
          assertTrue(format.isCommentMarkerSet()); // Expecting true since a comment marker is set
      }
    new_imports_code: ''

          CSVFormat format = CSVFormat.DEFAULT.withEscape(BACKSLASH);
          format.printRecord(output, "Value with newline\nand comma,");
          String expectedOutput = "\"Value with newline\\nand comma,\""; // Expecting the newline to be escaped

      Test behavior when header is set to null.

      entry_hint: |
        public CSVFormat withSkipHeaderRecord(boolean skipHeaderRecord)

        public void printRecord(final Appendable out, final Object... values)

      @Test
      public void testParseWithCustomRecordSeparator() throws IOException {
          String csvContent = "Value1;Value2;Value3\nValue4;Value5;Value6";
          File tempFile = File.createTempFile("test", ".csv");
          Files.write(tempFile.toPath(), csvContent.getBytes());
          CSVFormat format = CSVFormat.DEFAULT.withRecordSeparator(';');
          CSVParser parser = CSVParser.parse(tempFile, Charset.defaultCharset(), format);
          List<CSVRecord> records = parser.getRecords();
          assertEquals(2, records.size());
          assertEquals("Value1", records.get(0).get(0));
          assertEquals("Value2", records.get(0).get(1));
          assertEquals("Value3", records.get(0).get(2));
          assertEquals("Value4", records.get(1).get(0));
          assertEquals("Value5", records.get(1).get(1));
          assertEquals("Value6", records.get(1).get(2));
          tempFile.delete();
      }
